### [non-spec.21.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: CODE-6 item 18.2 keys `SubprocessExecutor`'s spawn-time `Serve` on "a tap is recorded" and drops the spawn-origin condition — BECAUSE the tap is the only consumer that needs the eager reader, and the only tapper (the adapter, via `installActivityTap`) never calls `Send`, so the tap predicate separates the two stdout readers while spawn origin does not — ALTERNATIVES: origin AND tap (the reviewer's wording; the origin conjunct is redundant once the tap gates it); Send reading through the hub (rewrites the dev-loop `readResponse` and its EOF-to-RUNTIME_CRASH path, outside this proposal); rewriting the Start-then-Send tests (they pin §28.5.3 session_start behaviour, and the two-reader hazard stays latent)
FACT: `Send` on a `Start`-spawned child reuses it (`session` returns the published child regardless of origin) and reads `sess.stdout`, a scanner over the same pipe as `stdoutPipe`; an eager `Serve` on that pipe steals the `response` — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/executor/subprocess.go:172-173, :304, :413-414, :432-436
WATCHOUT: `TestSubprocessExecutorStartSpawnsProcess`, `TestSubprocessStartSpawnWritesNoSessionStart_spec_28_5_3`, and the tier-3 `TestSubprocessSessionStartValidatesAgainstSchema_spec_28_5_3` call Start then Send with no tap; they are the regression guard for the untapped path and must pass unchanged — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/executor/subprocess_test.go:124-146; pkg/gateway/session/executor/subprocess_session_start_test.go:111-120; tests/tier3_contract/adapter_jsonl/subprocess_session_start_test.go:85-93
CORRECTS [non-spec.20 DECISION at review-log "SubprocessExecutor only for a `Start`-spawned child"]: spawn origin does not separate `Send`'s scanner from the hub reader; the gate is the recorded tap.

### [non-spec.21.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: close both G2 findings by adding one Tier-1 test entry each under "Tests that land with CODE-6" (after `TestCheckHeartbeatBusy_spec_15_4_6`) and appending both names to the CODE-6 item 18 "Pinned by" list; item 18.4/18.5 prose unchanged — BECAUSE no existing or listed test discriminates either change (echo-concurrent tests send only unaddressed heartbeats; probe tests iterate `checkLevel` itself) — ALTERNATIVES: relying on tier-10 `TestHeartbeatBusyConformance_spec_15_4_6` (tier-10 batteries run only echo/streaming-echo/delegation-echo, and the check passes a serial runtime, so it pins neither ordering nor the unheld branch); widening the existing `TestSessionLifetimeIsABasicCategory_spec_15_4_6` (wrong name for the case, and it is landed 0090 test text).
WATCHOUT: the discriminating echo-concurrent assertion is the unheld/ended-session branch: `rejectUnheld` drops non-message frames silently, so a heartbeat routed there goes unanswered. EVIDENCE: cmd/runtimes/echo-concurrent/dispatch.go `rejectUnheld`; dispatch.go heartbeat branch answers bare ack today.

### [non-spec.21.review-mechanism.1]
FACT: The gateway's `adapterclient.Client.Attach` binds the stream with an envelope-free first `AttachRequest` (pkg/gateway/runtime/adapterclient/client.go:879-887), so the adapter's `Attach` subscribes to `rt.Output` (pkg/adapter/attach.go:60) before it writes any gateway message. Starting the hub reader at hub creation (CODE-6 item 18.2, r20) therefore drops no turn response on the gateway path, even though `Attach` writes a first-frame envelope before it subscribes (attach.go:53-60) and `linefanout.Hub.broadcast` drops lines with no subscriber (pkg/adapter/linefanout/linefanout.go:116-126). EVIDENCE: client.go:879-887, attach.go:53-60.
WATCHOUT: Before filing a lost-frame race against the eager reader, check who sends the first Attach frame. Only a caller that sends an envelope on the first `AttachRequest` would race, and the gateway does not do that. The awaited `session_start` subscribes before it writes (pkg/adapter/sessionframes.go:352-362). EVIDENCE: sessionframes.go:352-362.
FACT: The bundled Basic-battery runtimes (echo, streaming-echo, delegation-echo, binary-minimal scaffold) handle a `message` synchronously in their frame loop. Their bare `heartbeat_ack` therefore always follows the `response`, so they pass the `heartbeat_busy` row unchanged. echo-concurrent is the only bundled runtime that needs the r20 routing edit. EVIDENCE: cmd/runtimes/streaming-echo/main.go:130-140, cmd/lenny-ctl/runtimescaffold/templates/binary-minimal.main.go.tmpl:295-303, tests/tier10_conformance/scaffolds_test.go:106-109.
FACT: In production the tenant registry is wired, and `requireSessionQuota`-style tenant lookups read it on every cap check (pkg/gateway/sessionserver/quota.go:23-35). So `admitResume` errors during a primary failover whatever the cap settings are, and the Edge-cases pointer to CODE-7 item 4 for a `suspended` entry row holds. EVIDENCE: quota.go:23-35.

### [non-spec.25.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: CODE-6 item 18.1 **Clock.** now stages a `Server.clock func() time.Time` field (tests set it) and a nil-safe `now()` method falling back to `time.Now()`; every staged `s.now()` site is unchanged — BECAUSE `NewGRPCServer` only wraps a built Server, and `adapter.New` (server.go `New`) and struct literals (`&adapter.Server{WorkspaceBase: base}` in pkg/gateway/sessionserver/upload_to_session_test.go) leave a field nil, so only a nil-safe accessor covers every construction path — ALTERNATIVES: default in `New` (struct literals stay nil); `New` default plus accessor (redundant second mechanism); keep field `now` with a `clock()` accessor (rewrites every `s.now()` site; Go forbids a field and method of one name).
FACT: the adapter package's existing nil-fallback clock is `SessionUsageMeter`: field `now`, method `clock()` returning `time.Now()` when nil — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/usage.go `SessionUsageMeter`, `func (m *SessionUsageMeter) clock()`. Server has no existing `clock` or `now` member, so the staged names do not collide.
WATCHOUT: `heartbeat_external_test.go` is an external-package test (`adapter_test`) and cannot set the unexported `clock` field; the "injected clock" in that test bullet needs an `export_test.go` setter or the case moves to the internal test. Not edited here (outside G1's adjudicated sites) — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md Tier 1 bullet naming `heartbeat_internal_test.go` and `heartbeat_external_test.go` "with an injected clock".

### [non-spec.25.fix-G2.1]
DECISION: bound the leader backlog pass by keyset paging (`ListPodlessSuspended(ctx, tenantID, afterID, limit)`, PageSize 500) and a bounded fan-out of the existing `HeldLen` (Concurrency 32, `errgroup.Group.SetLimit`); index 0183 becomes `(tenant_id, id)` — BECAUSE suspended rows are exempt from every cap (CODE-7 item 1), so a serial unpaged pass over ~1.2M-6.6M podless rows overruns the 300 s SPEC-5i interval and materializes GBs on the leader — ALTERNATIVES: batch/pipelined HeldLen (rejected: four new methods across Coordinator, DLQ, RedisInbox, MemoryInbox for ~4k cmd/s); narrow projection (still one slice); restating the invariant as one pass duration (weakens SPEC-5i); operator flags for page size/concurrency (not operator-visible; interval flag is the knob).
WATCHOUT: `sessions.id` is UUID (migrations/0001_initial_schema.up.sql:75), so `id > ''` is a type error; the first page must omit the cursor predicate. Item 16.2 states this in one clause. Also use plain `errgroup.Group` (not `WithContext`) so one row's error does not cancel the page's other `HeldLen` calls. — EVIDENCE: go.mod:59 golang.org/x/sync; pkg/embedded/stack/logs.go:23 existing errgroup use.
CORRECTS [Standing context **Leader capacity.**]: "issues one `HeldLen` per row per 300 s" stays true, but the pass is now paged (one page of rows held) with at most 32 `HeldLen` calls in flight, so the pass-duration and leader-memory question that review-log UNVERIFIED (pass over uncapped population in 300 s) raised is closed by CODE-6 item 16.2/16.3 and pinned by the extended `TestBacklogRecheckerResumesPodlessHeld_spec_7_2` and `TestListPodlessSuspendedUsesPartialIndex`.

### [non-spec.25.fix-G4.1]

WATCHOUT: any reader-facing restatement of idle-window activity must carry SPEC-5ab's frame exclusions; "frames are activity" unqualified implies heartbeat traffic keeps a session running — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-5ab activity list ("other than `heartbeat` and `session_end`"), SPEC-5ac item 3.

### [non-spec.25.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: CODE-6 item 18.1 **Clock.** becomes a `clock func() time.Time` field plus a nil-safe `now()` method returning `time.Now()` when `clock` is nil; every `s.now()` site stays as written — BECAUSE the nil fallback is the package's existing pattern (`SessionUsageMeter.clock`, pkg/adapter/usage.go:104-110) and covers every construction path — ALTERNATIVES: defaulting in `NewGRPCServer` (current text; nil on `adapter.New`-built servers that call handlers directly); defaulting in `New` plus a nil-safe accessor (reviewer's fix; the `New` default is redundant once the accessor falls back); defaulting in `New` alone (misses struct literals).
FACT: `adapter.Server` is built as a struct literal outside `New`, e.g. `&adapter.Server{WorkspaceBase: base}` at pkg/gateway/sessionserver/upload_to_session_test.go:99, so a clock defaulted in any constructor is nil there — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/server.go:425-431, pkg/adapter/transport.go:27
WATCHOUT: Go forbids a field and a method of the same name; the field is `clock` and the method `now` so the `s.now()` call sites at items 4 and 18 need no edit. Summary table row 0084 lists the `Server` additions and must name `clock`.

### [non-spec.25.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: the leader backlog re-check pass is bounded by keyset paging of `ListPodlessSuspended` (afterID, limit, `id` order; index 0183 becomes `(tenant_id, id)` partial) plus a bounded number of concurrent `HeldLen` calls per page through errgroup.SetLimit — BECAUSE paging bounds leader memory to one page and concurrency divides the per-row Redis round trip, with no new Coordinator, DLQ, or inbox method — ALTERNATIVES: a pipelined batch `HeldLen` (reviewer's suggestion) needs batch variants on Coordinator, DLQ (`CountAcceptedAfter`), RedisInbox and MemoryInbox for no throughput gain at ~4k cmds/s; a narrowed projection row still holds the whole population in one slice; widening the invariant to "one pass duration" contradicts SPEC-5i and is a spec change; deleting the re-check reverses the OD-P3 answer.
CORRECTS [Standing context **Leader capacity.** / **Refuted: the 7-day suspended population as a finding.**]: "the leader pass is indexed" answered the query cost only. The serial per-row `HeldLen` and the one-shot full-row listing did break the 300 s invariant at ~1.2M podless rows (~300 s of round trips, GB-scale slice). After this fix the refutation stands for the other 7(l)-class costs; refile the re-check only if paging or the concurrency bound is removed.
CORRECTS [review-log UNVERIFIED non-spec.8.review-performance.1]: resolved by this design (pass paged and concurrent).
WATCHOUT: the paged query must order by `id` with `id > $afterID` and the index must lead `(tenant_id, id)`; an index on `tenant_id` alone makes every page sort the whole podless set. The tier-2 EXPLAIN assertion is the discriminating one (index named, no Sort node). — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-6 item 16.1, test row TestListPodlessSuspendedUsesPartialIndex
WATCHOUT: do not loosen "a list error ... the pass continues" into a retry loop; a page list error ends that tenant's pass for this tick and the next tenant proceeds.

CORRECTS [review-log Standing context **spec-map rule.**]: the rule is now "the step that adds, deletes, or renames a tier-2+ test updates spec-map", stated in the Testing intro; compaction should replace the entry.

FACT: the intra-pod MCP server runs every tools/call on `context.Background()` (pkg/adapter/mcp/server.go:166-167), and `gatewaycontrol.Client.CallPlatformTool`/`CallConnectorTool` pass that ctx to a unary gRPC with no deadline; the LNK-GWCONTROL dial sets no client keepalive (pkg/adapter/gatewaylink.go:50-57, gatewaycontrol.go:61-63). DECISION: keep the string `afterID` cursor; pgstore omits the `id` bound when it is empty (CODE-6 item 16.2), and the tier-2 `TestListPodlessSuspendedUsesPartialIndex` asserts the empty-`afterID` first page — BECAUSE it changes no interface — ALTERNATIVES: pointer cursor (interface churn on both stores and the consumer), nil-UUID sentinel (magic value every caller must know), separate first-page query (parallel query to keep aligned with the index)
WATCHOUT: only the tier-2 pgstore case discriminates the empty-cursor bug; adding it to the memstore tier-1 test proves nothing — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md Testing, tier-2 `TestListPodlessSuspendedUsesPartialIndex`
CORRECTS [review-log WATCHOUT "the paged query must order by `id` with `id > $afterID`"]: holds for every page after the first; the first page (empty `afterID`) carries no `id` bound on pgstore.

### [non-spec.26.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: close the UUID first-page defect by replacing the parenthetical in CODE-6 item 16.2 with "pgstore omits the `id` bound when `afterID` is empty, because `''` is not a valid uuid", and adding one clause to TestListPodlessSuspendedUsesPartialIndex that a first call with an empty `afterID` returns the tenant's first `limit` rows — BECAUSE the signature stays string-typed and the query text stays IMPLEMENTOR'S CHOICE; only the empty-cursor semantics needed stating and pinning at the tier that runs pgstore — ALTERNATIVES: `*string`/nullable cursor (changes the consumer interface for no gain); nil-UUID sentinel `00000000-...` (magic value, still needs stating); a separate first-page method (second query to keep in sync with the index).

### [non-spec.26.review-performance.1]
DECISION: no performance or failure-mode finding in non-spec round 26 — BECAUSE the r25 delta (CODE-6 item 16 paging plus `Concurrency`, the adapter `clock`/`now()` default, the spec-map rule move, the SuspendIdle retry-gate test) adds no per-unit write. The paged leader pass costs one ZCARD per podless row under the default non-durable inbox, so at round 25's ~6.65M-row bound, 32 in flight and roughly 0.3 ms per call, a pass takes about 1 to 2 min, inside the 300 s interval — ALTERNATIVES: refiling pass duration under a 100%-idle-ending assumption (rejected: it rests on an RTT and an idle fraction the spec does not state, and the finding was fixed in r25); making PageSize and Concurrency flags (rejected: a code-practice nit, not a correctness defect).
FACT: `idx_sessions_podless_suspended (tenant_id, id)` serves `tenant_id = $1 AND id > $2 ORDER BY id LIMIT n` with no sort, and `sessions.id` is UUID, whose byte ordering matches lowercase-hex string order, so the memstore string cursor agrees with pgstore. — EVIDENCE: migrations/0001_initial_schema.up.sql:75; non-spec-changes.md:397-398
FACT: during a Redis blackhole each leader `HeldLen` takes about 12 s (go-redis defaults), so a pass stalls for the length of the outage. Once Redis returns, the rest of the pass finishes quickly and the next tick re-checks the skipped rows, so the delay is bounded by about one interval plus the pass time. This is not a finding. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:400-402
FACT: an unlifted seal left by a coordinator crash between the seal and the suspend write delays held messages by at most one idle window. A refused `message` neither stamps the adapter clock nor resets the §6.2 clock, so the adopter's next `sweepIdle` attempt seals, checkpoints, and releases. This is no worse than the shipped idle expiry. — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:475 (client bullet "a message the runtime adapter writes"), :801; non-spec-changes.md:337 (Refusal: no write and no stamp)
USEFUL [non-spec.25.review-performance.1]: its ~6.65M-row bound and its "one ZCARD per podless row in non-durable mode" FACT let the r25 fix be costed without re-deriving them.

### [non-spec.27.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: the CODE-4 existing-tests bullet now rewrites `TestStreamFailureConcurrentPoolDrainsAtThreshold_spec_5_2` to count reported sessions by `retryCount` rising over the pre-silence reading — BECAUSE the session uses the default 900 s window on a warmCount 2 pool, so the resume driver restores it to `running` before `settledSlotFailures` reads it — ALTERNATIVES: waiting for `running` drops the slot-failure-count assertion; a short window does not stop the claim; lowering warmCount changes a shared fixture.
FACT: `reportedSessions` feeds both `awaitAnySessionReported` and `settledSlotFailures`, and `sessiondriver.Session` lacks `RetryCount` although the session response carries `retryCount` — EVIDENCE: tests/tier5_e2e_kind/stream_failure_kind_test.go reportedSessions; tests/testinfra/sessiondriver/sessiondriver.go Session; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/session_response.go RetryCount.
WATCHOUT: any other tier-5 or higher test that asserts a session stays in `resume_pending` or `awaiting_client_action` on a pool with a spare warm pod breaks under CODE-4 the same way; grep for those states in tests/ when auditing the CODE-4 edit list — EVIDENCE: tests/tier5_e2e_kind/stream_failure_kind_test.go reportedSessions.

### [non-spec.27.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: close the concurrent-pool drain finding with one sentence appended to the CODE-4 "Existing tests that pin the synchronous `POST /resume`" bullet, after the exclusive-pool rewrite, retargeting `reportedSessions` in stream_failure_kind_test.go to a `retryCount` rise over a pre-silence reading — BECAUSE the helper feeds both `awaitAnySessionReported` and `settledSlotFailures`, so one predicate change fixes both waits, and `retryCount` survives the driver's restore while the row state does not — ALTERNATIVES: assert `running` on a pod other than the drained one (loses the slot-failure-count discrimination the test exists for); a short retryPolicy window (the driver claims a second warm pod inside 5 s, per the exclusive-test sentence, so it does not stop the restore); dropping warmCount to 1 in install.sh (shared fixture, changes other tests).
FACT: the public session response carries `retryCount` (pkg/gateway/sessionserver/session_response.go:59) but tests/testinfra/sessiondriver/sessiondriver.go:99 `Session` has no such field; the rewrite must add it. — EVIDENCE: as cited
WATCHOUT: a pod-loss end of the sibling slot also moves its row out of `running` and raises `retryCount`; today's state-based predicate already counts it the same way, so the retryCount predicate preserves current semantics rather than tightening them. Do not file that as a new defect of this sentence. — EVIDENCE: tests/tier5_e2e_kind/stream_failure_kind_test.go:384-399, :444-450

### [non-spec.29.review-client-surface.1]
DECISION: Returned no findings under the client-surface lens in non-spec round 29 — BECAUSE every client-facing change has its parallel representations staged: RESUME_FAILED (spec 04/05/15/29 via SPEC-3c/3g/3h/3o, errorclassify, podclaimerror, error-catalog docs; no SDK/schema/openapi names it), maxSessionRetries (openapi + generated_tools, runtimestore, admin, three docs pages; no SDK/CRD/chart names it), the deadline_approaching "idle" trigger (runtime-ops schema enum, proto comment, runtimeops.go, adapterclient, adapter-contract.md; no SDK names it), heartbeat/heartbeat_ack sessionId+busy (JSONL schema, adapter-contract.md, tier-3 address test at S8; Go/Python/TS runtime SDKs; tier-3 SDK test over all three languages; tier-10 check), interrupt_session via postByID (Go SDK, localcli, tier-3 consistency), and QUOTA_EXCEEDED on resume/messages (REST, MCP send_message tool error, resume_session via postByID, tier-3 error matrix, rest.md/mcp.md/error-catalog/multi-tenancy docs) — ALTERNATIVES: filing the off-holder held-pod /resume TARGET_NOT_READY details (currentState only, while the §29.3 lead says the refusal carries the coordinator's identity), rejected because the shipped upload off-holder answer (upload_to_session.go:117-120) already carries currentState alone, so the gap is tree-wide and pre-existing.

FACT: OpenAPI responses blocks are not exhaustive (POST /v1/sessions lists no 429), so a missing 429 on /resume or /messages is not a parity defect. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/externalapi/openapi/openapi.json /v1/sessions/{id}/resume responses {"200"}

### [non-spec.29.review-kubernetes.1]
USEFUL [Settled: Kubernetes surface.]: still holds at round 29. The only apiserver writes the non-spec staging adds are claim DELETEs through the existing `podclaim.DeleteClaim` (CODE-3 item 3), covered by the gateway's `sandboxclaims` create/get/delete grant (charts/lenny/templates/gateway-deployment.yaml:160-162). The backlog re-check registers on the existing leaderGate (cmd/lenny-gateway/leader_sweeps.go).
FACT: A gateway claim DELETE after `claimPod` always lands on a claim that already carries `bound`. `claimIdle` writes `bound` right after CREATE (pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/claimer.go:214-221), and a rebind patches `reserved → bound`. The projection therefore takes `claimed → draining` (pkg/controller/warmpool/occupancy.go:135-141), which fails closed. A failed handshake never returns the pod to `idle`. EVIDENCE: occupancy.go:104-109 (an unset binding leaves the phase untouched).
FACT: The orphan GC's "active session" lookup counts any non-terminal row, `suspended` included (pkg/controller/warmpool/gc.go:70-84). A held-pod `suspended` session older than `claimOrphanTimeout` is not drained. EVIDENCE: gc.go:79-83.

### [non-spec.29.review-mechanism.1]
FACT: The round-28 rewrite of `TestStreamFailureConcurrentPoolDrainsAtThreshold_spec_5_2` (count reported sessions by a `retryCount` rise) holds against the tree. The only tests that set `HeartbeatInterval` are pkg/adapter/heartbeat_external_test.go and tests/tier4_integration/stream_failure_report_test.go, and the proposal dispositions both. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-adapter/main.go:225-230
FACT: Every `RuntimeProcess` implementation (SocketRuntimeProcess, InProcessRuntime, MCPRuntime, executor.SubprocessExecutor) is on CODE-6 item 18.2's tap list, so `activityTapped` is true for every production runtime. Every runtime write other than heartbeat, session_start, and session_end goes through `writeSessionEnvelope`. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/attach.go:272-285, pkg/adapter/sessionframes.go:115,135, pkg/adapter/heartbeat.go:153
FACT: streaming-echo, delegation-echo, and elicitation-echo answer `heartbeat` with a bare ack from a sequential frame loop, so they never ack while a message is in flight and pass the `heartbeat_busy` check without edits. EVIDENCE: cmd/runtimes/streaming-echo/main.go:108-140
WATCHOUT: `deliverToSession` counts and logs an unaddressed-frame rejection itself. If `observeRuntimeFrame` (CODE-6 item 18.1) calls it rather than reusing only its resolve rule, `lenny_adapter_unaddressed_frame_rejected_total` gets one extra count per frame. The proposal says "resolves the entry as deliverToSession does", which permits either reading. This is an implementor-level trap and not a finding. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/attach.go:376-397
UNVERIFIED: The existing `sweepIdle` expiry mutator, which CODE-6 item 5 keeps for sessions that cannot be idle-suspended, returns nil on a non-running row and re-checks only the state. RECORDS-1 7(k) files the same pattern for two other sweeps but not this one. This is pre-existing and was not filed. The next RECORDS reviewer should decide whether 7(k) should name it. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/runtime/watchdog/watchdog.go:1020-1045

### [non-spec.29.review-performance.1]
DECISION: no performance or failure-mode finding in non-spec round 29 — BECAUSE the r27 delta is only the tier-5 concurrent-pool drain test rewrite, and the 7c21ce84f delta's new hot paths stay within settled bounds: the llmproxy per-line `Stamp` coalesces at 1 s per session (sessionidle.go:178-192), `StampAt` runs only on a `sealed: false` answer (once per idle window per session), `beginToolCall` is one `s.mu` hold per call, and `admitResume` issues no COUNT while every cap is disabled — ALTERNATIVES: filing the synchronous `admitResume` in `ResumeBacklog` as a Tick stall during a primary partition (rejected: `Tick` already runs `List`/`Update` on the long-lived context with no per-tick deadline, watchdog.go:588-617, so no new class of stall); filing concurrent idle-suspension checkpoints as unbounded (rejected: the steady-state bound is about 11/s at Tier 3, already in Settled **Per-message and idle costs.**)
FACT: `Stamper.pruneLocked` scans the whole coalescing map on each non-coalesced `Stamp` once the map holds 4096 or more entries (sessionidle.go:210-221). That is O(n^2) per second per replica when n or more sessions stamp each second. It predates 0092 (agent-output stamps follow the same path), and the per-chunk proxy stamp adds no uncoalesced calls — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/sessionidle/sessionidle.go:184-189
FACT: a readopted binding carries an `Adapter` client (cmd/lenny-gateway/coordination_seams.go readoptAndFence builds `BindResult{Adapter: adapter}`), so `SuspendIdle`'s "binding carries no adapter connection" no-op does not strand a session after a takeover — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/coordination_seams.go:163-250
FACT: treebudget keys are reclaimed only by TTL (PurgeRoot is called only from erasure, adminrouter.go:528; settle runs GETDEL only on the hwm key), so the [non-spec.1.review-performance.1] OPEN on 8x key retention is a property of the converged SPEC-5o TTL. A non-spec remedy would need a settle-time purge, which no spec section states — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/delegationtree/treebudget/treebudget.go:416-420, :474
USEFUL [non-spec.8.review-performance.1]: the ~11/s Tier-3 idle checkpoint and claim bound meant this round had no throughput to re-derive.

### [non-spec.29.review-reliability.1]
DECISION: no findings under the reliability lens in non-spec round 29 — BECAUSE the r27→r29 delta is only the tier-5 drain-test rewrite, and the 7c21ce84f delta (CODE-6 item 18 tool-call counting, per-entry heartbeats, SDK busy hold, llmproxy per-chunk stamp, CODE-7 admitResume) traced clean through crash, takeover, and store failure, or lands on outcomes SPEC-5ab/SPEC-5i already accept (stale seal holds messages in the DLQ until a later attempt, entry removal, or maxSessionAge; a persistently failing idle checkpoint leaves held entries waiting for the next delivery point) — ALTERNATIVES: filing the SuspendIdle step-3 store-error repeat as unpaced (same class as Traps **Commit repeat pacing.**, do not refile).
FACT: `sessionidle.Stamper.Stamp` is non-blocking (async `persist`, 5 s timeout, coalesced 1/s), so the CODE-6 item 5 per-chunk proxy stamp cannot stall the LLM relay during a Postgres outage — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/sessionidle/sessionidle.go (Stamp/persist).
FACT: a socket runtime write error is terminal (`p.ended`, conn per pod life), so a heartbeat monitor ending on a write error does not silently drop liveness for a still-live runtime — EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/socketruntime.go WriteEnvelope/Output.
FACT: no adapter code calls `WriteEnvelope` while holding `s.mu`, so the item 18 tap taking `s.mu` on the hub reader cannot deadlock against a blocked stdin write — EVIDENCE: awk scan of pkg/adapter/*.go.
FACT: a `DeadlineExceeded` Attach end is a reported stream failure (`isReportedStreamFailure`), so SPEC-5ac item 7's refusal of later Attach calls after a missed ack routes into the failure funnel rather than wedging the session — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/executor/podstream.go:364.
USEFUL [Traps **Commit repeat pacing.**]: saved refiling the identical SuspendIdle repeat.

### [non-spec.29.review-security.1]
FACT: The r27→r29 delta is one test-disposition bullet (TestStreamFailureConcurrentPoolDrainsAtThreshold_spec_5_2 rewrite); it has no security surface. EVIDENCE: diff of scratchpad/cp-snap/0092-redesign6/non-spec-r27-prefix against the proposal dir.
FACT: Tenant suspension drains through ForceTerminate over every non-terminal row, `suspended` included, so the backlog re-check and the driver cannot revive a suspended tenant's session. Resume paths need no TENANT_SUSPENDED gate. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/tenant_suspend.go:179-203
FACT: PodExecutor.Release drains an exclusive pod whatever the disposition, and a slot bind calls ReleaseSlot. The DispositionCompleted release in the idle and interrupt releases therefore never returns the pod for reuse by another session. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/executor/pod.go:257-271
FACT: The llmproxy per-chunk Activity stamp keys on lease.TenantID/lease.SessionID, which come from the gateway-side lease, not a pod header. The idle bound is not a security bound, because maxSessionAge active time accrues gateway-side in Store.Update (CODE-1). A runtime's `busy` or an adapter's idle_age therefore extends a session no further than that cap. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/llmproxy/llmproxy/handler.go:496-554
DECISION: No security findings this round. BECAUSE every bound I checked (caps via primary COUNTs failing closed, active age, credential release scoped by generation, seal RPCs outside the hold allowlist) has a trusted, durable source. ALTERNATIVES: filing a budget-key TTL lapse after a failed re-arm; rejected because it needs >8 days since the last successful arm and the standing context already records it as a spec property.

### [non-spec.31.review-kubernetes.1]
FACT: A gateway `podclaim.DeleteClaim` on a claim whose Sandbox sits in `claimed` drains the pod (occupancy projection claimed → draining); only a `reserved` pod returns to idle. So CODE-3 item 3's new claim deletes (handshake failure, exclusive-pool `releaseResumeSlot`, `failResume` after a failed adapter Resume) never re-pool an unscrubbed pod. EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/warmpool/occupancy.go:113-141; pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/claimer.go:433-444
FACT: The orphan-claim GC skips a claim while any non-terminal row's `pod_assignment` names the pod (`GetActiveSlotsByPod`), so a held-pod `suspended` row longer than `claimOrphanTimeout` is safe, and a crash between the pod-clearing write and the release (interrupt release or `SuspendIdle`) is drained by the GC. EVIDENCE: pkg/controller/warmpool/gc.go:223-247; cmd/lenny-controller/main.go:168-186
FACT: `gatewayleader.Gate` runs each job on a leader-scoped context that client-go cancels on loss, so CODE-6 item 16's `Run` (Pass at start, then ticker until ctx ends) matches the existing job contract. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/coordination/gatewayleader/gatewayleader.go:191-229

Double admitResume on the REST and MCP resume paths was rejected because it is a redundant count and does not affect correctness.

### [non-spec.31.review-security.1]
FACT: The tenant-suspend drain force-terminates every non-terminal session, `suspended` included, so a podless `suspended` session cannot be backlog-resumed for a suspended tenant. §11.4 full revoke cancels the user's non-terminal sessions, and soft and hard disable deny only new sessions, so a resume needs no user-gate check. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/tenant_suspend.go:173-203; spec/11_policy-and-controls.md:250-270
FACT: The creation per-user cap counts by the principal's `Subject` (create.go:99-104), but the row's `user_id` is the body's `userId` (create.go:241). CODE-7's `admitResume` keys on `row.UserID`, the same column the count reads, so the resume check is no weaker than creation. The Subject-versus-body mismatch predates 0092. Do not file it against 0092. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/create.go:99-104, :241; pgstore.go:880-891
FACT: `beginToolCall` keeps the `callingSession` exclusivity gate, so a call is refused unless exactly one session resolves. The adapter has no per-method auth allowlist (only `coordinatorHoldAllowedMethods` and `credredact`), so the seal RPCs need no auth entry. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/platformtoolprovider.go:69-76, pkg/adapter/runtimegeneration.go:162-170, pkg/adapter/holdstate.go:53-55
DECISION: Filed nothing. BECAUSE: under the security lens, every bound the delta touches is gateway-authoritative and fails closed. The cap COUNTs run on the primary and answer 500 on error. Active age is a Postgres column. The `idle_age_seconds` that SealSession returns feeds only a forward-only stamp, which a lying adapter could already obtain by answering not sealed. A runtime's `busy` affects only idleness, and `maxSessionAge` still bounds it. ALTERNATIVES: filing `IdleSuspendable`'s skip-on-resolve-error (a session whose runtime record is missing is never idle-reclaimed). It is a reclamation gap and not a security control, so a mechanism lens should look at it if anyone does.

### [non-spec.33.review-security.1]
FACT: The r31-prefix to r33 delta is RECORDS-1 items 8 (deferral R19 with a `deferral_id` key) and 9 (the plan tick wording). Neither has a security surface. EVIDENCE: diff -ru of scratchpad/cp-snap/0092-redesign6/non-spec-r31-prefix against the proposal dir, non-spec-changes.md:572-573
DECISION: Filed nothing. The llmproxy stamp keys on the gateway-side lease (pkg/gateway/llmproxy/llmproxy/handler.go:496-554). SPEC-6a states the cap exclusion of a held-pod `suspended` session ("whether or not it holds its pod", spec-changes.md:838), and that is an owner decision. ALTERNATIVES: (1) The Edge-cases "Concurrent-pool counter after an ambiguous commit" under-count lets a pod exceed `maxConcurrentSessions` by one until rehydration, against the §5.2 **Slot assignment atomicity** wording (spec/05:592). Rejected: it is an explicitly accepted, rare, same-tenant capacity overshoot (concurrent pods are tenant-pinned) rather than an isolation bound. (2) The MCP `QUOTA_EXCEEDED` details expose a global `active` count to an agent runtime. Rejected: SPEC-6a mandates the same details creation returns, and the case is hypothetical hardening. (3) Idle suspension or expiry never fires while `SealSession` errors (`seal_unavailable` is logged at INFO). Rejected: SPEC-5f states the outcome, and idle reclamation is not a security control, because `maxSessionAge` active time still bounds the session.

### [non-spec.35.review-kubernetes.1]
FACT: The proposal adds no CRD field, status condition, finalizer, ownerReference, or webhook change; every new state lives in Postgres or Redis. The gateway's Kubernetes writes stay the existing SandboxClaim create/delete and sandboxclaims/status patch (charts/lenny/templates/gateway-deployment.yaml:149-170), so CODE-3's added `podclaim.DeleteClaim` calls are within the gateway grant. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podclaim/claimer.go:433
FACT: The orphan-claim GC's active-session oracle is `GetActiveSlotsByPod`, which counts every non-terminal row naming the pod. A held-pod `suspended` row therefore protects its claim, and a podless row (after the release's guarded clear) leaves the claim to be drained on the next GC tick. Deleting a claim whose pod projects `claimed` drains the pod, so the release and failure paths fail closed. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/sessionstore/pgstore/pgstore.go:742-757; cmd/lenny-controller/main.go:180; spec/04_system-components.md:415
FACT: The backlog re-check registers on the existing gateway `leaderGate` (cmd/lenny-gateway/leader_sweeps.go:84), so it adds no new Lease or RBAC. No synchronous request path waits on a controller reconcile: a podless resume writes `resume_pending` and the driver claims the pod asynchronously.

SPEC-5f ("enqueues it in the session's DLQ ... when ... the adapter refuses the re-routed message again") accepts this, so do not file it as a stranded message. — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:499

### [non-spec.35.review-performance.1]
FACT: a refused seal (`sealed == false`) costs one `SealSession` RPC plus one uncoalesced `StampAt` row write per idle window per session. The stamp is strictly forward: the row is over-idle (now - rowLAA >= W) and the adapter is not idle (now - adapterLA < W), so the stamp lands later than the row's value, and it uses durations only, so clock skew between gateway and pod does not matter. A busy or tool-calling session therefore re-arms once per W and does not churn every 5 s tick. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-6 item 4 **`SuspendIdle`.** step 1; pkg/gateway/session/sessionidle/sessionidle.go `persist` (forward-only Update).
FACT: `Stamper.persist` drops its Update error, so during a Postgres failover a refused seal's stamp is lost and the holder retries `SealSession` each 5 s watchdog tick until the primary returns. The RPC is bounded and causes no store churn. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/sessionidle/sessionidle.go persist `_, _ = s.store.Update`.
FACT: adapter default heartbeat interval is 30 s with a 10 s ack timeout, so the new per-entry monitors (CODE-6 item 18.3) add negligible pod-local load. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-adapter/main.go:225-230.
FACT: no current `pkg/adapter` path holds `s.mu` across `WriteEnvelope`, `Interrupt`, or `Checkpoint`, so the tap taking `s.mu` per runtime frame (item 18.1) cannot stall the hub reader behind runtime I/O. EVIDENCE: awk scan of pkg/adapter/*.go non-test files.
WATCHOUT: a seal left by a coordinator that crashed between step 1 and step 3 stays while the runtime keeps reporting activity, because a later attempt that gets `sealed == false` stamps and does not unseal. Messages then wait in the DLQ until the runtime goes idle for W and the adopter's attempt completes. This is the staged SPEC-5ab design ("A seal that its attempt does not lift stays until..."), so it is not a non-spec finding. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:801.

- FACT: treebudget keys are created only by `Reserve` (delegation) and `GrantTokenBudget`; a root with no children has none, so `RearmTree`'s "only when a parent exists" skip leaves no budget key to expire. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/delegationtree/treebudget/treebudget.go:172-195, 443-457

### [non-spec.37.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: close the CH-RUNTIMEOPS activity gap in CODE-6 item 18.1 with one new setting-site bullet (`RuntimeOps.readLoop` calls an observer for every `llm_request_completed`) — BECAUSE SPEC-5ab's read bullet already covers it and the spec intent ([spec.26.review-fresh.1]) reads it that way — ALTERNATIVES: scoping SPEC-5ab's read bullet to CH-MSGSOCK (spec edit, and it would make a bare-acking direct-mode runtime sealable mid-work); reusing `tokenSink` (it is called only for non-zero token counts, and it is the usage concern).

### [non-spec.37.review-reliability.1]

FACT: `sessionidle.Stamper.Stamp` is non-blocking. It coalesces to one write per second per session and persists in a goroutine under a 5 s timeout, and it drops failures. The CODE-6 item 5 per-chunk `flush` stamp therefore cannot stall an LLM stream during a Postgres failover. A dropped `StampAt` on the `SuspendIdle` not-sealed branch only means the next watchdog tick asks the adapter again, so it is not a stampede. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/sessionidle/sessionidle.go:178-192, :227-239
FACT: Client messages do not stamp `last_agent_activity_at`. Only agent-activity events published through `publishEvent` do, along with the LLM-proxy usage stamps. A sealed `running` session whose refused messages sit in the DLQ is therefore re-attempted by the next idle firing, and the client's sending does not hold it off. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/events.go:360-370, cmd/lenny-gateway/llmproxy_usage.go:316,:474
FACT: A held message stranded in the DLQ of a `running` session after a Lift or a checkpoint-failure unseal is spec-accepted. SPEC-5i **Delivery of held messages.** says it "waits for the next delivery point or the session's expiry". Do not file it as a reliability defect. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-5i **Delivery of held messages.** (the sentence "When no message arrives, the idle suspension ... deliver it, except while the idle suspension does not complete")
FACT: Runtime soft delete keeps `runtimestore.Get` answering, because pgstore `Get` has no `deleted_at` filter. As a result `IdleSuspendable`'s resolve error is transient only, and a deleted runtime does not leave an over-idle row skipped forever. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/runtime/runtimestore/pgstore/pgstore.go:282-293, :398-404
WATCHOUT: Each failure arm of `openRuntimeSession` releases the claim, so a second `session_start` on the same registry entry, which would find a stopped non-nil `entry.hb` and start no monitor, has no path in the tree. Before filing that as a missed monitor restart, confirm that a caller reuses the entry. EVIDENCE: pkg/adapter/session.go:183-199, pkg/adapter/resume.go:171-181, pkg/adapter/sdkwarm.go:277-287

### [non-spec.37.review-security.1]
FACT: The round-37 delta adds only the wiring test for the llmproxy per-chunk stamp. `newLLMProxyServer` returns `&http.Server{Handler: proxyMux}` with no middleware wrapper, so asserting through `ServeMux.Handler` is feasible — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/llmproxy_server.go:33-60
DECISION: no security finding — BECAUSE every bound the delta touches comes from a trusted source. The per-chunk stamp keys on the gateway-held proxy-mode lease (TenantID and SessionID), and it fires only in proxy mode. A pod's `busy` or seal refusal can defer only idle suspension, which is a resource bound. Active-time `maxSessionAge` still accrues while the session is `running`. The cap counts are primary-pool Postgres and fail closed. The idle-suspension release takes the pool's normal session-end disposition, so recycle and scrub are unchanged — ALTERNATIVES: filing runtime-controlled `busy` as an attacker-influenceable bound was rejected, because idle suspension is not a security property and the age cap stays gateway-measured.

### [redesign1.apply.2]
FACT: Applied every edit of the 2026-10-11 redesign 1 reconciled edit list (edits 1 to 63, which merge review-log compaction pass 7, the idle-activity inventory, and the delegated answers to OD-N1 to OD-N5). Every anchor matched once, and no edit was skipped. Edit 29 is a no-op by design: SPEC-2d item 3 stays as written, and its §29.3 clause lands as SPEC-5p2 item 2.
DECISION: The idle-activity setting sites are redesigned as one inventory. CODE-6 item 18.1 now holds a table of adapter rows A1 to A7 (which set `lastActivity` and `toolCalls`) and gateway rows G1 to G9 (which advance `last_agent_activity_at`), and declares a write of either clock outside the table a defect. SPEC-5ab item 2 states the adapter activity list as closed and adds `llm_request_completed`. CODE-6 items 4 and 5, four summary watch-outs, Decision 16, and the DOCS-1b runtime-author bullet point at the table instead of restating it — BECAUSE the non-spec loop stopped for the idle-activity inventory: the setting sites were stated in several places and no list was closed.
DECISION: OD-N1 to OD-N5 are answered under the owner's standing delegation of 2026-10-09. The summary lists no open decision, and `### Open` holds no question.

### [spec.30.fix-G1.1]

WATCHOUT: never paraphrase "activity" in SPEC-5ab or the Edge cases; the bulleted inventory in SPEC-5ab **Session seal.** is its only definition. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-5ab item 2.

The rule it guards is unchanged.

### [spec.30.fix-G3.1]
DECISION: SPEC-5p2 is now one unnumbered edit (the §9.2 **Elicitation Timeout Semantics:** item 6 insert), and item 6 is the one home of when a resolution is enqueued and of the paths on which it is lost — BECAUSE the deleted §29.3 row clause restated item 6 unconditionally, enqueued for a still-waiting child of a podless root (double delivery), and covered tool-use approvals — ALTERNATIVES: the reviewer's "when the call has ended" and "for an elicitation" qualifiers lost because each is a second copy of item 6's condition in the row.
WATCHOUT: Do not re-add an enqueue clause or a "no call is blocked on a pod" premise to the §29.3 interaction-resolution row (SPEC-2d item 3). A child of an idle-suspended podless root can still be waiting in `lenny/request_elicitation`; its call polls the interaction store and reads the recorded resolution, so the replica forwards nothing and enqueues nothing. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/mcptools/mcptools_register.go (the `deps.Interactions.Get` poll loop in the request_elicitation handler).
WATCHOUT: Rejected: a durable reclaim (an `enqueued` column plus a restore-time or leader re-check sweep) to make held-resolution delivery reliable. It lost because without an observation that the wait ended it cannot tell a live child's wait from an ended one, and the loss paths were accepted. Delivery is at most once.
FACT: The gateway control gRPC server sets no server keepalive and `GracefulStop()` runs with no deadline, so a pod that dies without closing its stream is never observed and the resolved branch never calls `EndWait`. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/runserver.go (`w.gatewayCtrlSrv.GracefulStop()`); keepalive appears only on the client dial side.
CORRECTS [review-log WATCHOUT "Replaced: the SPEC-2d item 3 clause citing §9.2 ... the clause is SPEC-5p2 item 2"]: SPEC-5p2 item 2 is deleted. The §29.3 row carries no clause citing item 6 at all, which also removes the S2/S5 dangling-citation concern.

CORRECTS [review-log Standing context, "SPEC-5ac item 2 stays a pointer." paragraph]: it quotes the old SPEC-5ab sentence "Work a runtime begins without emitting a frame or making a platform or connector call ..."; after round 30 the home sentence reads "Work a runtime begins after the session's last activity and before the seal without producing activity ...". ### [spec.30.fix-design-G3.1]
DECISION: SPEC-5p2 item 1 (§9.2 **Elicitation Timeout Semantics:** item 6) becomes the one home of the paths on which a held resolution is not enqueued, by one sentence after "enqueues each resolution at most once." — BECAUSE the non-spec Edge cases bullet already accepts those losses and only the landing spec text promised otherwise — ALTERNATIVES: a durable reclaim (an `enqueued` column plus an `EndWait`-style sweep at restore commit or a leader re-check) lost: a new mechanism, and it still cannot tell a live child's wait from an ended one without the observation the losses lack.
DECISION: SPEC-5p2 item 2 is deleted, and SPEC-2d item 3 drops "no call is blocked on a pod and". The §29.3 row then says only who records the resolution; §9.2 item 6 alone says when it is enqueued. This closes the double delivery to a running child (finding 4) and the tool-use approvals enqueued as elicitation resolutions (finding 5) with one deletion.
FACT: an elicitation's interaction row is keyed to the resolver (root) session, and `Detail["originPod"]` names the raising session. The waiting `request_elicitation` handler polls `Interactions.Get` in Postgres, so a resolution that a non-coordinator replica records reaches a still-live child's call with no forward. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/mcptools/mcptools_register.go:1600-1615; elicitation.go:140-145
FACT: the gateway's control gRPC server sets no server keepalive (the only keepalive is the gateway-to-adapter client dial), and shutdown calls `GracefulStop()` with no deadline. A pod that dies silently therefore leaves the handler waiting until `maxElicitationWait`, and a respond that arrives in that window returns into a dead stream without `EndWait`. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/runserver.go:222-224; cmd/lenny-gateway/pod_lifecycle.go:215
OPEN: the base §29.3 interaction-resolution row says the root's coordinator "resolves the pending ... elicitation the pod is blocked on". For a child's elicitation the blocked pod belongs to the child, and the code resolves it by Postgres polling rather than by a forward. This is a pre-existing spec wording gap and is not this proposal's edit.
WATCHOUT: do not re-add an enqueue clause to the §29.3 row, in any state-qualified or kind-qualified form. Item 6 is the home, and the row covers tool-use approvals too.

### [spec.30.review-fresh.1]
FACT: `retryPolicy` is per-session client-settable and `ClassifyFailure` replaces the default lists with the client's when non-empty, so a client `retryableFailures` naming `credential_rejected` classifies it retryable (the non-spec test `TestClassifyFailureCredentialRejected_spec_7_3` asserts exactly this). Any spec sentence calling a failure reason unconditionally non-retryable is false under an override; SPEC-1b item 3 correctly defers to the classifier, SPEC-1g did not. — EVIDENCE: spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:386 ("set per session by the client"), :400; pkg/api/v1/session/retry_policy.go:375-405; spec/08_recursive-delegation.md:947 (a client moving `runtime_crash` to `nonRetryableFailures`)
FACT: The SPEC-5ab activity inventory matches the §28.5.3 CH-RUNTIMEOPS message table: only `llm_request_completed` among runtime-to-adapter frames carries `sessionId`; `checkpoint_ready`, `interrupt_acknowledged`, `credentials_acknowledged` carry only correlation ids, and `lifecycle_support`/`llm_request_started` are process-scoped. The §28.3 channel register's intra-pod channels are exactly CH-MSGSOCK, CH-RUNTIMEOPS, CH-MCP-PLATFORM, CH-MCP-CONNECTOR; CH-LLMPROXY is pod-egress and is a gateway qualifying event. — EVIDENCE: spec/28_communication-channels.md:114-131, :1177-1199 (message table)
FACT: The §29.3 interaction-resolution row (spec/29_communication-scenarios.md:574) covers tool-use approve/deny as well as elicitation respond/dismiss, so any sentence edited into it must scope elicitation-only behavior to the elicitation routes.
FACT: §4.9's session-start deliveryMode admission check exists (spec/04_system-components.md:1628, last sentences) and says "its pod is bound"; the code evaluates the pool match's profile (credential_preclaim.go:357), which is known before a claim, so SPEC-1g running it pre-claim is workable. Not filed.
DECISION: did not file the §9.2 item 3 per-hop forwarding timeout against SPEC-5p2 item 6 (item 3's "treats it as a failure" may or may not resolve the elicitation) — BECAUSE the reading is ambiguous and below the bar — ALTERNATIVES: filing it as an unreachable/unbounded held elicitation.
USEFUL [spec.29.review-applicability.1]: the whitespace-collapse anchor note; the SPEC-1h JSON anchor resolves at spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:396-397.

### [spec.30.review-kubernetes.1]

The only Kubernetes-facing statements are SPEC-1b's orphan-GC citation (spec/04_system-components.md:515-517, WarmPoolController leader, predicate 1 keyed on the claim's last binding-state transition and Postgres `pod_assignment`), SPEC-5b step 2's release disposition (gateway-owned `SandboxClaim` binding state, spec/04_system-components.md:619, :634-639), and SPEC-5i **Backlog resume.**'s use of the `lenny-gateway-leader` Lease (spec/12_storage-architecture.md:358, 25 s failover bound). All three match the tree's ownership and leader-election rules.
FACT: `SealSession`/`UnsealSession` (SPEC-5ab) are gateway-to-adapter gRPC on CH-ATTACH; seal state lives on the adapter registry entry, never on a CRD. A Kubernetes lens has nothing to file there — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-5ab item 2.

### [spec.30.review-mechanism.1]
FACT: The per-session retry lists are client overrides, not additions: `ClassifyFailure` replaces the default `retryableFailures`/`nonRetryableFailures` with a non-empty client list (pkg/api/v1/session/retry_policy.go:376-399), and docs/runtime-author-guide/delegation.md:489 shows a client moving `runtime_crash` to non-retryable. Any spec sentence that states a failure reason's §6.2 edge outright (rather than "the classifier selects") is false under an override. EVIDENCE: retry_policy.go:376-399; non-spec TestClassifyFailureCredentialRejected_spec_7_3 asserts FailureRetryable under a client list naming credential_rejected.
FACT: An elicitation's interaction is keyed on the resolver session (the root for a human-facing chain), and the raising session's `lenny/request_elicitation` call polls the interaction store by that key, so a resolution recorded on any replica reaches a live child's call without a forward. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/mcptools/elicitation.go:140-145, :451-511; mcptools_register.go:1596-1650.
WATCHOUT: The §29.3 resolution row for a podless `suspended` session (SPEC-2d item 3) says "no call is blocked on a pod"; that is false when the root is the resolver of a running child's elicitation, and SPEC-5p2 item 2's enqueue then reads as delivering the resolution twice (call result plus held message). EVIDENCE: spec-changes SPEC-2d item 3, SPEC-5p2 item 2; elicitation.go:451.
USEFUL [Settled **Adapter seal.**]: the inventory check against §28.3 (CH-MSGSOCK, CH-RUNTIMEOPS, CH-MCP-PLATFORM, CH-MCP-CONNECTOR) and `sessionScopedFrameTypes` (pkg/adapter/attach.go:305-312) found SPEC-5ab complete and agreeing with the CODE-6 table rows A1 to A7.

### [spec.30.review-reliability.1]
FACT: The `lenny/request_elicitation` handler polls the interaction store and returns a resolved phase on its resolved branch without knowing whether the caller is still connected; under CODE-6 item 19.3 that branch never calls `EndWait`. A resolution recorded after the pod died, but before the gateway notices the dead stream, is returned into the dead stream and is never held. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/mcptools/mcptools_register.go:1601-1615 (resolved and dismissed branches), :1617-1619 (ctx.Done branch)
FACT: The GatewayControl gRPC server, which serves platform calls, sets no server keepalive, so it notices a dead peer only through TCP keepalive. Shutdown runs `GracefulStop()` with no deadline, so a waiting elicitation call keeps the drain open until the kubelet kills the pod, and `EndWait` never runs. — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/runserver.go:222-224; the only keepalive in cmd/lenny-gateway is a client option at pod_lifecycle.go:215
WATCHOUT: SPEC-5p2 item 6 says the gateway holds and enqueues the resolution whenever the call ends. The staged mechanism loses it on a replica stop, a drain, an undetected dead pod, and an enqueue or re-read failure after the respond commits. The non-spec Edge cases bullet **A held elicitation resolution that is never enqueued.** accepts most of these losses, but the spec states none of them, and the undetected-dead-pod path is missing from both. Filed as [spec.30.review-reliability] finding 1.
DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md]: The Edge cases bullet **A held elicitation resolution that is never enqueued.** omits a loss path. When a respond lands while the gateway has not yet noticed that the raising pod died, the handler's resolved branch returns the result into the dead stream, and nothing is enqueued. That path should be added if the spec fix states the losses.

### [spec.30.review-security.1]
FACT: Tenant suspension terminates every non-terminal session, `suspended` included, so a backlog resume or resume driver cannot bring back a session of a suspended tenant through a path that skips the drain. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/tenant_suspend.go drainTenantSessions (ForceTerminate over every non-terminal row); spec/15_external-api-surface.md:848.
FACT: §12.8 processing restriction (GDPR Art. 18) gates only the session-creation endpoints and lets in-flight sessions finish. A resume of a `suspended` session during erasure is therefore not a bypass of a stated control. EVIDENCE: spec/12_storage-architecture.md:807.
FACT: The SPEC-1g lease-generation rule returns every lease on some release: finalize and mid-session leases record 0, restore leases record the entry generation, and renewals inherit the replaced lease's value, so each one is at or below some later binding stamp. No credential leak path. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-1g.
UNVERIFIED: SPEC-5p2 delivers a held elicitation resolution, which is client-authored content, as a `message` with `from.kind: system`, and §15.4 reserves `system` for platform-injected messages (spec/15_external-api-surface.md:1587). No spec text tells runtimes to trust `system` content, so this was not filed. A messaging or client-surface lens should decide whether `system` is the right provenance label for that message.

### [spec.30.review-single-source.1]
DECISION: filed two findings — (1) SPEC-5ab's "Work a runtime begins without emitting a frame or making a platform or connector call ... a release ... ends it" paraphrases the activity predicate, and after this run's inventory rewrite the paraphrase disagrees with it (an `llm_request_started`, a `busy: false` ack, `session_started`, or an unaddressed frame on a multi-slot pod is a frame and not activity), so SPEC-5f's "Session seal states the work a release can still end" leaves that work unstated; fix is to key the sentence and the Design edge-case label on "without activity"; (2) SPEC-1g restates the post-claim failure-reason mapping that SPEC-1b item 3 owns (Settled **Post-claim failure reason.**): "The driver reports it as `credential_rejected`" and "is reported as `runtime_crash`" — BECAUSE the delta is the only new text since round 29 and both are stating sites a reader could implement from alone — ALTERNATIVES: filing SPEC-5ab's six-type session-scoped read list as a copy of §28.5.3 **Addressing.** (rejected: Traps forbid citing Addressing for the tap, and the inventory is meant to enumerate; the two extra types `message`/`tool_result` are adapter-to-runtime only and so vacuous in a read bullet); filing SPEC-5p2 item 6 "the check governs only the resume" against SPEC-6a "neither held nor delivered" (rejected: an explicit carve-out for a resolution, which reaches no client route SPEC-6a names); filing SPEC-6e's "is not counted" (one-clause consequence of the §11.1 count).
FACT: the inventory's CH-RUNTIMEOPS claims check out: only `checkpoint_request`, `checkpoint_complete`, `interrupt_request`, `credentials_rotated`, `deadline_approaching`, `files_updated`, and `llm_request_completed` carry `sessionId`; `lifecycle_support`, `llm_request_started`, and the runtime replies do not. — EVIDENCE: spec/28_communication-channels.md:1177-1186
FACT: the intra-pod and pod-egress channels in the register are CH-MSGSOCK, CH-RUNTIMEOPS, CH-MCP-PLATFORM, CH-MCP-CONNECTOR, CH-LLMPROXY, and CH-OBJSTORE; SPEC-5ab names the first five and covers the rest with "other channels ... not adapter activity". — EVIDENCE: spec/28_communication-channels.md:124-128, :1425
USEFUL [spec.29.review-single-source.1]: its not-filed inventory (SPEC-5f/SPEC-5ab conjunction pointer, backlog predicate, admission, lease retention) still holds after the delta.

### [redesign1.apply.3]
FACT: Applied the 2026-10-11 elicitation-expiry redesign (scratchpad/redesign/0092_...-redesign-1.md, edits 1 to 28). Every anchor matched once, every counted replacement matched its stated count (0185 to 0184: 8 in non-spec-changes, 1 in summary, 6 in Standing context), and no edit was skipped. Edits 13 and 27 make no change to staged text by design.
DECISION: Redesigned one area, the outcome of a pending `lenny/request_elicitation` elicitation when its session leaves its pod — BECAUSE the held resolution (answer B to OD-N4) lost the resolution in the pod-failure case it was chosen to fix, cited a §8.2 rule that holds the elicitation rather than its resolution, and delivered client text as `from.kind: system`. SPEC-5p2 item 6 now states the expiry, CODE-6 item 19 implements it as `interactionstore.Store.ExpireRaisedBy` keyed on `detail.originPod` and called from `ReportSessionFailure` after a committed edge and from `ReleaseSuspendedPod` step 4 under `slotAccountLocks`, and a later respond or dismiss gets `ELICITATION_NOT_FOUND` through the existing `ErrNotFound` paths.
DECISION: Deleted from the change files: migration `0184_interactions_wait_ended`, `Interaction.WaitEnded`, `Store.EndWait`, `HoldElicitationResolution` and its `SessionDelivery` method, `sessioninbox.Message.SenderKind` and its `senderFrom` mapping, the `elicit_<id>` held message, every item-19 edit to `elicitation.go` and `interactions_resolution.go`, the tests `TestEndWaitSerializesWithResolve_spec_9_2`, `TestElicitationWaitEndHoldsResolutionOnce_spec_9_2`, `TestHoldElicitationResolutionRoutesByState_spec_9_2`, `TestHoldElicitationResolutionAtCapStaysHeld_spec_9_2`, and `TestHeldElicitationDeliveredAfterSuspensionRelease_spec_9_2` with `held_elicitation_test.go`, the `maxElicitationWait` Non-goal, the `EndWait` Watch-out, and the Edge-cases bullet on a resolution never enqueued.
DECISION: Reconciled outside the edit list: summary Decision 33 drops the redesign's parenthetical on why answer B lost, because the summary states what to build and that history is recorded here. The sweep for `wait_ended`, `WaitEnded`, `EndWait`, `HoldElicitation`, `SenderKind`, `elicit_<`, "held elicitation", `maxElicitationWait`, `held_elicitation`, `interactions_resolution`, and `from.kind` found no other hit in the summary, checklist, spec-changes, or non-spec-changes. The `senderFrom` hits that remain are CODE-4's move of the helper into the executor package, which is unrelated. Checklist S15 needs no dependency change, and S16 loses only the deleted cap test.
CORRECTS [redesign1.apply.2] "OPEN: OD-R1 (answer to OD-N4), default B applied": closed as OD-E1. Answer A generalized replaces answer B: every pod-leaving transition expires the session's pending `lenny/request_elicitation` elicitations, and a later respond or dismiss gets `404 ELICITATION_NOT_FOUND` (Decision 33, SPEC-5p2, CODE-6 item 19).
CORRECTS [redesign1.apply.2] "CODE-6 item 19's `interactions` migration takes 0184 (S15), and CODE-7's counted-session index moves to 0185 (S16)": CODE-6 item 19 has no migration, and CODE-7's counted-session index takes 0184.

CORRECTS [redesign1.apply.2] WATCHOUT "Replaced: answer A to OD-N4": answer A generalized to every pod-leaving transition is adopted as OD-E1 (Decision 33); the WATCHOUTs on the CapRefusal clause and the SPEC-5p2 hold no longer apply.
OPEN: OD-1 (CODE-7's migration number after 0184 is freed), default A applied: CODE-7's counted-session index is renumbered to 0184 so migrations stay contiguous. Option B keeps CODE-7 at 0185 and leaves 0184 unused; it reverses the 0185-to-0184 replacement in non-spec-changes, summary, and Standing context, and rewrites the second CORRECTS line above to "0184 is left unused, and CODE-7 keeps 0185."

  It lost because a pod failure before the gateway noticed the dead stream lost the resolution, the §8.2 rule it cited holds the elicitation rather than its resolution, and it labelled client text as platform text.

  The expiry makes the row correct as it stands: a podless `suspended` session's own elicitations no longer exist, so `resolveInteraction` returns 404 first, and the remaining rows are child elicitations whose call polls the store.

### [spec.31.review-performance.1]
FACT: SPEC-1g3 adds one lease-store read per proactive renewal (credleasestore is Postgres-backed, pkg/gateway/credentials/credleasestore/pgstore/pgstore.go). A renewal already mints and writes a replacement lease (spec/04_system-components.md:1543-1546), so the read does not change the renewal's order of cost. FACT: The SPEC-5p2 elicitation expiry costs one DELETE per pod release or committed failure edge (non-spec CODE-6 item 19.1, keyed on detail->>'originPod' and read through idx_interactions_tenant_user). At the Tier 3 idle-suspension rate of about 11/s this load is negligible. How much a tenant and user with many interaction rows costs per DELETE is a code question for the non-spec loop, not a spec finding. — EVIDENCE: 0092 non-spec-changes.md:464
DECISION: no findings this round. BECAUSE: the new text (the elicitation outcome, SPEC-1g, and SPEC-1h) adds no per-unit-of-work etcd write, no hot key, and no single-leader path. SPEC-1g's pre-claim credential check runs at most once per driver backoff (10 s, CODE-4 item 12). The expiry and the lease return each say what happens on a store failure: SPEC-5p2 says "one whose expiry fails stays pending", and a lease the release does not return expires on its TTL. ALTERNATIVES: filing the per-message SPEC-6a COUNTs or the loss of held DLQ messages on a Redis reset. Rejected because both are already recorded in Standing context (**Cap counts.** and the MISTAKE entry on store-outage refutation).

### [spec.32.review-reliability.1]
DECISION: no reliability findings in spec round 32 — BECAUSE the r31 delta (SPEC-1h clause (d) pointer to §11.2 **Irrecoverable trees:**, and SPEC-5p2 dropping the §7.3 replay-window clause and re-pointing the child clause to §8.2 **Replay on resume:**) adds no recovery path; a whole-document re-trace of the new text found every crash/restart window closed or already accepted: SPEC-1g leases of a crashed pre-commit attempt are returned by the next pod release (generation ≤ that binding's stamp) or lapse on TTL; SPEC-5p2 expiry runs after the release, and a resolution recorded in that gap is the stated "no runtime receives it" residual; an elicitation raised after the seal is pending at release and is expired — ALTERNATIVES: rejected (1) BUDGET_STATE_UNRECOVERABLE moving a `suspended` root out unchecked against the caps (admission/capacity lens, and the "coordinator lost" trigger is ambiguous for a podless root); (2) a transient post-claim credential-pool race reported as `runtime_crash` and spending a retry (design choice, bounded by maxRetries → awaiting_client_action); (3) a SealSession reply lost after the adapter sealed (Settled **Not-sealed keeps the seal.**); (4) a failed budget-key re-arm (rejected before, review-log line ~1183).
FACT: §8.2 **Replay on resume:** is trigger-neutral ("on parent resume") and §29.6 emits `children_reattached` on every restore, which SPEC-3k routes the podless-suspended resume through, so SPEC-5p2's child-elicitation pointer holds for a release restore. EVIDENCE: spec/08_recursive-delegation.md:112; spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:160; spec/29_communication-scenarios.md:1165

### [spec.35.review-mechanism.1]
DECISION: filed one finding: the r34 SPEC-5q rescoping of `lenny_session_resume_attempts_total` to "every restore attempt of the §7.3 resume driver" counts pre-claim claim failures, because SPEC-1b **Resume driver.** calls a failed claim an attempt ("An attempt proceeds in this order: 1. The driver claims ..."; "the driver attempts again later"), while CODE-4 item 3 steps 5 and 6 increment nothing before the `resuming` entry write — BECAUSE the spec metric and the staged code disagree on granularity, and under pool exhaustion the spec reading yields one `failure` per backoff tick (default 10 s) for the whole window — ALTERNATIVES: making the code count pre-claim failures (rejected: inflates the failure side with retries of one wait and is a non-spec remedy).
FACT: The elicitation expiry (SPEC-5p2) holds against §7.3 **External side effects across recovery.** and §8.2 **Pending elicitations:**/**Replay on resume:**: the expiry key is the raiser, a child's elicitation stays held, and the re-raise after restore is an ordinary re-issued platform-tool action. The §7.3 at-least-once paragraph is scoped to the failure restore path, which predates 0092 for podless `suspended` restores; not filed. — EVIDENCE: spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:426-430, spec/08_recursive-delegation.md:111-112
FACT: The §4.9 session-start `deliveryMode` check is evaluated in code against the pool match (`checkCredentialDeliveryIsolation(match, ...)`), so SPEC-1g running it before the claim on the pinned pool is workable despite §4.9's "its pod is bound" wording. Not filed. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/finalize.go:266-276, spec/04_system-components.md:1628
WATCHOUT: §25 (spec/25_agent-operability.md:4097) says a session with a missing workspace snapshot "transitions to `failed` on next resume attempt", while §6.2 sends a non-retryable `resuming` failure to `awaiting_client_action`. Pre-existing; not filed.

### [spec.35.review-performance.1]
DECISION: no performance or failure-mode finding in spec round 35. BECAUSE the only spec-changes delta since the r33 snapshot is the SPEC-5q `lenny_session_resume_attempts_total` row rewording. That row is a gateway-local Prometheus counter that keeps its labels (`pool`, `outcome`), and no §16.5 alert or SLO reads it (grep of spec/16_observability.md finds only the §16.1 row at :28), so the rewording adds no store write and no cardinality. ALTERNATIVES: filing the ambiguous-commit case of the idle-suspension write, which errors after a landed COMMIT and releases nothing, so the pod waits for claimOrphanTimeout. Rejected because the shipped graceful release (SPEC-5b step 2) has the same write-then-release pattern, and Standing **Commit witness.** already records the pgstore behavior. The design therefore degrades no worse than the shipped one.
FACT: A Postgres outage between the seal and the idle suspend write leaves a sealed `running` session that has no lifting call, because the attempt unseals only on a checkpoint failure or a condition miss. Refused messages go to the DLQ. The next clock firing still sees the old `last_agent_activity_at`, the adapter keeps the seal and answers sealed, and the write lands after failover. The session then suspends, and the releasing replica's Backlog resume moves it to `resume_pending`. Recovery takes one restore, which is better than the shipped idle expiry. — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-5f **Idle suspension.** (line 513), SPEC-5ab item 2 **Session seal.**
USEFUL [spec.31.review-performance.1]: its SPEC-1g3 read cost and its SPEC-5p2 DELETE rate meant this round had nothing to re-derive.

### [spec.35.review-reliability.1]
DECISION: no reliability findings in spec round 35 — BECAUSE the r33→r35 delta is only the SPEC-5q `lenny_session_resume_attempts_total` rewording (anchor verified at spec/16_observability.md:28) and re-tracing SPEC-1g, SPEC-1h, and SPEC-5p2 under crash, restart, and concurrent restore found every window closed or already recorded as accepted — ALTERNATIVES: filing the SPEC-5p2 over-expiry race (a late `ExpireRaisedBy` keyed on the raising session deletes an elicitation that a restored runtime re-raised); rejected because the release site runs under `slotAccountLocks` before any same-replica claim, and a cross-replica restore needs the lease to lapse first, so the window is too narrow to meet the bar.
WATCHOUT: SPEC-5p2 expires by raising session rather than by pod or generation. If a later change moves the expiry off the release lock, or makes the failure-edge expiry asynchronous, it can delete a restored runtime's re-raised elicitation. In that case scope the key to the departing incarnation. — EVIDENCE: proposals/0092_new_write-the-session-suspend-and-resume-proposal-that-gateway/0092_new_write-the-session-suspend-and-resume-proposal-that-gateway.non-spec-changes.md:464-466 (ExpireRaisedBy on detail.originPod = session ID)

### [spec.35.review-security.1]
DECISION: no security findings in spec round 35 — BECAUSE the r33->r35 delta (SPEC-5q `lenny_session_resume_attempts_total` scope) is metric wording only, and a whole-document re-read under the security lens found no regressed control: SealSession/UnsealSession ride the existing gateway→adapter CH-ATTACH link (no new pod egress, no apiserver path); `busy` is an untrusted runtime report but bounds only idle reclamation (gateway clock is the other conjunct, maxSessionAge bounds it), never a security limit; SPEC-1g lease generations are gateway-recorded; credential_rejected is non-retryable (fail closed to awaiting_client_action); the resume admission is a Postgres COUNT that admits nothing on failure; the tenant pin is read on every idle-pod acquisition including the resume claim (spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:448); §11.4 full revoke cancels every non-terminal session, so a podless `suspended` session is covered (spec/11_policy-and-controls.md:258-268) — ALTERNATIVES: (1) a resume from entry path (d) restoring onto a replacement pod while the root's old pod stays bound with live leases — rejected as pre-existing (§29.6 step 4 already claims a replacement pod for every awaiting_client_action resume, spec/29_communication-scenarios.md:1060-1068) and a §11.2 gap ([spec.32] WATCHOUT); (2) suspended sessions with held pods escaping the concurrent-session caps — owner decision OD-S10/Decision 27, "less strict" is not a finding.
FACT: §28.5.3 already states the adapter resolves every CH-MCP-PLATFORM/CONNECTOR call to the single session the shared runtime process was given and refuses otherwise, so SPEC-5ab's "activity for exactly one session" premise holds — EVIDENCE: spec/28_communication-channels.md:1371, :1864

### [non-spec.38.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: the SPEC-5q `lenny_session_resume_attempts_total` scope names what the driver observes after a successful entry write (commit landed = success; reported failure or commit not landed = failure) — BECAUSE the driver cannot tell whether an item 3.6 hook store error applied the write, and a shutdown-cancelled attempt has no outcome, so "write took effect" was unemittable — ALTERNATIVES: adding failure increments at item 3.6 and at shutdown (guesses an outcome); "releases its uncommitted pod" wording (would sweep in the CODE-3 item 3 claim release after a hook error).
DECISION: CODE-4 item 5.3 **Landed above `G`.** now runs step 5's success counter and `session.resumed` audit row, matching the item 5.4 relinquish case — BECAUSE a landed commit is a success whichever replica holds the pod, and CODE-5 adoption is not a driver attempt, so nothing else counts it.
WATCHOUT: spec-changes.md was edited this round (SPEC-5q resume-attempts replacement text only) — EVIDENCE: SPEC-5q, sentence beginning "In the `lenny_session_resume_attempts_total` row".
CORRECTS [the OPEN near review-log line 2064 that describes the SPEC-5q scope as attempts whose entry write "took effect"]: the scope is now driver-observed outcomes after a successful entry write; a DOCS-1b runbook bullet derived from it must use that wording.

### [non-spec.38.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: the SPEC-5q `lenny_session_resume_attempts_total` scope moves from "every attempt whose entry write took effect" to the outcomes the driver observes after a successful entry write: `success` when it observes its item-4 commit take effect, `failure` when it reports a failure (item 3) or observes the commit not take effect; and CODE-4 item 5.3 **Landed above `G`.** runs step 5's success counter and `session.resumed` audit row, as the relinquish case in item 5.4 already does — BECAUSE the driver cannot know whether an errored entry write (item 3.6) applied, nor count an attempt the shutdown context cuts off, so "took effect" is unemittable; and a landed commit is a success whoever ends up holding the pod — ALTERNATIVES: counting item 3.6 and shutdown cancellations as failure (rejected: 3.6 may not have applied, and a cancelled attempt has no outcome; both add emitter branches); leaving Landed-above-G uncounted (rejected: a resume whose commit landed is then counted by nobody, since CODE-5 adoption is not a driver attempt).
WATCHOUT: "releases its uncommitted pod" is the wrong failure predicate: the binder also releases the claim after an item-3.6 hook store error (CODE-3 item 3), which must stay uncounted. Phrase failure as "observes the commit not take effect". EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-4 item 3.6 and CODE-3 item 3.
CORRECTS [spec.35.fix-design-G1.1]: its WATCHOUT "the population is entry write took effect … do not narrow it" no longer holds; the population is now attempts whose entry write succeeded and that reach an observed outcome (commit landed, failure reported, or commit not landed).
CORRECTS the OPEN carried at review-log `[spec.37...]` line ~2064: a DOCS-1b rewording of docs/runbooks/pod-kill-during-session.md:74 must use the round-38 SPEC-5q scope, not "write took effect".

### [non-spec.38.review-mechanism.1]
- FACT: The round-37 SPEC-5q rewrite scopes `lenny_session_resume_attempts_total` to "every resume driver attempt whose `resume_pending → resuming` write took effect". The CODE-4 emitters count only three outcomes: step 3.7 (reported failure), item 5.3 not landed (failure), and item 5.5/5.4 relinquish (success). Three entered-incarnation outcomes count nothing: item 3.6 store error that applied, item 5.3 "Landed above `G`", and **Driver goroutine at shutdown.** EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:141, :156, :852; spec-changes.md:716
- WATCHOUT: "Landed at `G`" plus relinquish count success, but "Landed above `G`" (also a landed commit that a takeover then owns) counts nothing. Check both whenever the metric scope is edited. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:155-156, :163
- FACT: Elicitation expiry key verified: `request_elicitation` writes `detail.originPod = row.ID` (the raiser) and `UserID = row.UserID`, with `SessionID` set to the resolver. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/mcptools/mcptools_register.go:1510-1538
- FACT: `isCredentialRejection` inputs verified. `mapAssignError` turns gRPC NotFound into `ErrPoolNotFound` and ResourceExhausted into `ErrPoolExhausted` (credassign/client.go:428-436). The adapter `AssignCredentials` RPC error is not wrapped in `CredentialAssignmentError` (podsession/credentiallease.go:53), so it stays `runtime_crash`. EVIDENCE: as cited
- FACT: The CODE-7 SLI clause holds. `exportSessionAvailabilityRatio` divides the recovery count (`resume_pending`, `resuming`, `awaiting_client_action`) by `CountActiveSessionsGlobal`. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/metrics_exporters.go:146-165; pgstore.go:952

### [mechanism.resume-attempts-counter.1]
DECISION: `lenny_session_resume_attempts_total` is specified as one mechanism: one deferred `recordResumeAttempt` per `attemptResume` and the closed exit table in CODE-4 item 25. The counter sentences in items 3.6, 3.7, 5.3, 5.4, and 5.5 and in the tier-1 driver test bullets are replaced by references to item 25. SPEC-5q states the same population in one sentence citing SPEC-1b **Resume driver.** items 2 to 4. BECAUSE the scattered increment sites disagreed: **Landed at `G`.** ran step 4, whose relinquish branch runs step 5's success counter, and then ran step 5's success counter again, which double-counts. ### [spec.38.review-security.1]
DECISION: filed one security finding, SPEC-1g3 replacing the §4.9 renewal step 1 session-liveness stop with a lease-record-only stop — BECAUSE the spec states no rule that every session end releases the shared lease record (the only release statement is §7.1 step 23, a flow step), so a session that ends without that release (a terminal write on a replica other than the one holding the renewal heap) keeps being renewed under the staged text, where the current text stops it — ALTERNATIVES: deferring to Settled **Renewal liveness.**, rejected because that entry answers a code-regression framing (the shipped renewal never checked liveness) and does not address the spec dropping the stated stop condition.
FACT: §4.9 renewal step 1 reads "**Check session liveness.** If the session owning the lease has ended, remove the lease from the heap and skip renewal." and no §4.9 Credential Leases text ties session end to a lease-record release. EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:1543, spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:54
FACT: Tenant suspension drains every non-terminal session, `suspended` included, so the leader backlog re-check cannot restore a session of a suspended tenant. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/tenant_suspend.go:173-179, spec/15_external-api-surface.md:848
FACT: Budget-key expiry fails closed at spec level (sentinel `EXISTS` check returns `BUDGET_KEYS_EXPIRED`), so SPEC-5o's TTL is no silent reset of a delegation budget. EVIDENCE: spec/08_recursive-delegation.md:408

On the rebuild path `Prepare` wraps the assign error with %w (podsession/bindprepare.go:176). A revoked user credential returns `credentialstore.ErrNotFound` (usercreds/usercreds.go:186-188). EVIDENCE: as cited
