### [spec.39.review-performance.1]
DECISION: no performance or failure-mode finding in spec round 39 — BECAUSE the only spec-changes delta against HEAD is SPEC-5i **Delivery of held messages.** gaining the adoption delivery point ("after it adopts a `running` session together with the session's pod") plus the commit-writer-stops-or-loses-lease handover, and SPEC-5i2 item 1 naming the coordinating replica as the deliverer. The adoption point adds one inbox and one DLQ peek per adopted `running` session, issued only on the §10.1.2 takeover path that already performs a Postgres CAS per session, so a mass takeover at Tier 3 adds Redis reads well under the CAS cost and no store write, etcd write, watch, or scan. Its predicate matches SPEC-2a's adoption set (a `running` row that names a pod). A commit-writer crash before delivery now degrades to delivery after lease TTL plus the 15 s Sweeper interval (about 75 s), which is no worse than the shipped behavior, where no held-delivery point existed at adoption — ALTERNATIVES: filing at-least-once redelivery when the commit writer delivers partially and then loses the lease (rejected: Settled **Held-message delivery.** "no at-least-once sentence", and it is not a performance defect); filing the Postgres-failover case where the commit lands but `Update` errors, leaving held entries until the next delivery point (rejected: the bound is the idle window via idle suspension plus Backlog resume, and the code-side commit repeat is CODE-4 item 5.2, so the remedy is not in the spec staging).
USEFUL [spec.38.review-performance.1]: its whole-proposal costing (idle claim churn, leader pass, cap COUNTs, budget re-arm, SPEC-1g3, SPEC-5p2) still holds, so only the adoption-delivery delta needed costing.

### [spec.39.review-reliability.1]
DECISION: no reliability findings in spec round 39 — BECAUSE the only spec-level statement of a commit-to-running tail effect is SPEC-5i **Delivery of held messages.** (SPEC-1b item 4 is reduced to a pointer by SPEC-5i2 item 1, and SPEC-3p step 14 points at item 4), and its writer/adopter split ("delivers after that write only while it is still the session's coordinating replica ... the replica that adopts the session delivers") matches every column of the non-spec CODE-4 item 5 **Post-commit tail.** table: Landed delivers, Relinquished and Above `G` leave it to an adopter whose §10.1.2 step 0 read found `running` (SPEC-2a adopts no `resuming` row, so an adopter can only have read the post-commit row), and Context ended leaves it to the adopter of the landed row — ALTERNATIVES: filing the Context-ended skip of `children_reattached`/`session.resumed`/`recoverDelegationTree` against §7.2 "exactly once per parent resume" (rejected: no spec staging is its remedy, and it equals the pre-existing crash-after-commit window; OD-H2 owns who runs those rows).

Needs a store error on the commit plus a lease loss plus a state move inside the lapse window; not filed. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-5i **Backlog resume.**, non-spec-changes.md CODE-4 item 5 table `deliverBuffered` row

### [spec.41.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: SPEC-5f **Idle suspension.** not-sealed branch now "advances `last_agent_activity_at` to the adapter's last activity when that is later" — BECAUSE an unconditional set could regress the row past a gateway-only qualifying event (attach, elicitation response, CH-LLMPROXY chunk) stamped between the sweep's read and the write, and the suspend write's `last_agent_activity_at` condition would then match the regressed value — ALTERNATIVES: conditioning the suspend write on both clocks or re-reading the row (adds a mechanism; forward-only guard suffices); also rewording the refusal branch's "current time" set (out of scope; only clock skew could make it earlier).
FACT: `sessionidle.Stamper.persist` is forward-only (`if r.LastAgentActivityAt.Before(at)`), so the spec text now matches code and CODE-6 row G9 `StampAt`. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/sessionidle/sessionidle.go:227-234
WATCHOUT: the refusal branch in the same paragraph still says "sets `last_agent_activity_at` to the current time"; that is deliberate (not part of this finding), do not file it as the same defect without a clock-skew argument. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-5f **Idle suspension.**

### [spec.41.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: SPEC-5f **Idle suspension.** not-sealed branch becomes "the gateway advances `last_agent_activity_at` to the adapter's last activity when that is later and writes nothing else" (replace in place) — BECAUSE the unconditional "sets" lets a write regress the row past a gateway-only qualifying event (attach, elicitation response, CH-LLMPROXY chunk) that landed between the sweep read and the stamp, after which the suspend CAS matches the regressed value; the landed `sessionidle.Stamper.persist` and CODE-6 G9 `StampAt` are already forward-only, so this aligns spec to code — ALTERNATIVES: making the suspend CAS compare against both clocks (larger, new mechanism); rewording the refusal branch ("current time") too (not filed; now is not earlier than a stamped event except under replica clock skew, a separate question).
WATCHOUT: review-log FACT at the 1475 region ("the stamp is strictly forward") argues forwardness from row-vs-adapter ages at sweep time; that argument ignores a concurrent gateway-clock stamp between read and write, which is exactly this finding. The forward-only guard is what closes it, not the age arithmetic.

### [spec.42.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: SPEC-5i **Delivery of held messages.** no longer restates which mechanism delivers a held DLQ entry when no message arrives; it points at the idle suspension and **Backlog resume.** "as each states", plus the session's expiry — BECAUSE the old sentence claimed Backlog resume delivers the entry, but for an interrupt suspension (InterruptAcknowledged/InterruptTimeout) the backlog predicate counts only DLQ entries accepted after entering `suspended`, and the idle clock is paused in `suspended`, so the entry waits for a client message/resume or expiry — ALTERNATIVES: widening the backlog predicate (changes settled behavior); adding an interrupt exception (second copy of the predicate).
CORRECTS [review-log.md:1600 FACT and :2367 DECISION alt 1]: the quoted sentence "When no message arrives, the idle suspension ... and **Backlog resume.** deliver it, except while the idle suspension does not complete" is gone; the behavior those entries record is unchanged and the delivery bullet now defers to each mechanism's own predicate.

### [spec.42.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: SPEC-5i **Delivery of held messages.** closes the no-message case by replacing the sentence "When no message arrives, the idle suspension (§6.2) and **Backlog resume.** deliver it, except while the idle suspension does not complete ..." with one that defers to each mechanism's own predicate ("the entry waits for the next delivery point that the idle suspension (§6.2) or **Backlog resume.** provides as each states, or for the session's expiry") — BECAUSE the old sentence restated Backlog resume's coverage and overclaimed it for a non-Idle suspension (interrupt), where the predicate counts only DLQ entries accepted after `suspended` and the idle clock is paused — ALTERNATIVES: widening the **Backlog resume.** predicate to count every DLQ entry for interrupt reasons (a behavior change round 41 already declined; the entry is not lost); adding an interrupt exception clause next to the idle one (hair: a second restatement of the predicate).
WATCHOUT: review-log FACT at line ~1600 and DECISION at ~719 quote the removed phrase "except while the idle suspension does not complete" as evidence; the accepted behavior (a stranded entry waits for the next delivery point or expiry) still holds under the new sentence, so compaction should repoint the quote rather than reopen the finding. EVIDENCE: review-log.md:719, :1600

### [spec.45.review-reliability.1]
DECISION: no reliability finding in round 45 — BECAUSE the only change since r43 narrows SPEC-1b's attempt-side release to snapshot-close step 3. Step 1's immediate cancel still holds on the attempt's own replica: there the cancelled RPC fails the attempt, and its guarded failure write finds the incarnation ended and runs step 3. On another replica the restore runs until the attempt's next write (summary Decision 21, accepted). Every crash window between seal, checkpoint, suspend write, migration, and release has a stated reclaimer: orphan-claim GC, the leader backlog re-check, or credential lease TTL lapse. — ALTERNATIVES: re-filing the stuck-seal and no-answer seal cases was rejected as settled (**Not-sealed keeps the seal.**, review-log line 380).
FACT: the idle-suspension checkpoint is bounded by the §4.4.3 60 s deadline, and a failure records a retry time (non-spec CODE-6 item 4, release step 2). A failing object store therefore does not get one idle-suspension checkpoint per watchdog tick. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:349
FACT: the resume driver's pre-claim retry is backed off and jittered (`now + backoff + jitter`, `--resume-driver-backoff-seconds`, default 10). — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:137, :212

### [non-spec.42.review-performance.1]
DECISION: no performance or failure-mode finding in non-spec round 42 — BECAUSE the r40-prefix delta is summary Decision 21 rewording, the new OD-T2 entry, and the retitled edge case **Terminal transition during an attempt.**; none adds a store write, a watch, a sweep, or a lock. Option A (staged) keeps a terminated `resuming` attempt's claimed pod until its next write, bounded by `MaxResumingSeconds`, which is the same bound OD-3 already accepted cross-replica; a mass terminate (for example §11.4 full revoke) therefore holds at most the in-flight restores' pods for one attempt deadline, not a new pool drain — ALTERNATIVES: filing the CODE-5 item 2c / CODE-4 item 26 takeover hook as fan-out amplification (rejected: it fires once per takeover publish, adds one ReadPrimary and two Redis peeks per adopted row beside the existing per-row RecordHandoff UPDATE and fence RPC, so it is a constant factor on adoption, and its failure paths log WARN and leave entries for the next SPEC-5i delivery point).
FACT: the coordination Sweeper has no per-sweep adoption cap; adoption is paced only by the jittered per-row backoff (minAdoptionBackoff..maxAdoptionBackoff 16 s). The item 26 goroutines therefore scale with the number of rows adopted in one sweep after a replica loss. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/coordination/coordination/coordination.go:111-117, :624
USEFUL [Standing context Leader capacity.]: rechecked the 1.4 ms HeldLen bound (6.65M rows / 300 s = ~22.2k rows/s at 32 in flight gives 32/22.2k = 1.44 ms); it holds, so the leader pass needed no re-derivation.

### [non-spec.45.review-mechanism.1]
DECISION: no findings under the end-to-end mechanism lens in non-spec round 45. BECAUSE the r43 to r45 delta (CODE-4 item 2 binderless landed test keeps the store-error conjunct; the tier-1 driver bullet points applied-then-errored exits at `TestResumeDriverPostCommitAndHookOutcomes_spec_7_3`) agrees with item 5.3, the item 25 exit table, and the tail test's Cases bullet. Each client-visible tail effect (status_change(running), session.resumed, children_reattached, held delivery) fires at most once per commit on every column. Relinquished and Above `G` deliver only through the adopter's item 26 hook. ALTERNATIVES: the landed-then-terminal corner of item 5.3's "handled as landed" invariant, rejected because it was already declined ([non-spec.44.review-single-source.1] WATCHOUT, [spec.43.review-mechanism.1]). The binderless baseline taken from the listed (read-pool) row, rejected for the reason in [non-spec.44.review-fresh.1].
CORRECTS [non-spec.44.review-single-source.1] UNVERIFIED: a Sweeper does run with no pod binder whenever Redis is wired. `coordination.NewSweeper` is built in cmd/lenny-gateway/redis_quota.go:248 without regard to `podBinder`, and only the readopt seam fails closed on a nil binder (cmd/lenny-gateway/coordination_seams.go:164). Under CODE-5, therefore, a binderless `resume_pending` row can receive a `RecordHandoff` bump (podHeld is false, so no readopt). The binderless commit guard is `State == resume_pending` alone, so a plain commit still lands. Only the store-error miss path reaches item 5.3's comparison with `G`, which item 2 never defines. The table note ("**Above `G`** do not arise") and the tier-1 binderless clause ("run the **Landed** column ... once") are what tell the implementor to take Landed there. Not filed, because the note plus the pinning test bound it. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:122-125, :179, :711
FACT: `Fencer.fence` fences at the row's current `coordination_generation`, which is not necessarily `G`, and the adapter accepts only a strictly greater value after the first fence (pkg/gateway/coordination/coordfence/coordfence.go:141-170, pkg/adapter/coordination.go:124-133). A resumer that fences after a peer's `RecordHandoff` but before the peer's fence therefore wins the fence at G+1 and takes **Landed**. The peer then relinquishes, and the next sweep evicts the resumer's G-stamped binding (peerTookOver), which heals the condition. This needs a lease lapse between the commit and the fence. Not filed as speculative.

### [non-spec.45.review-security.1]
FACT: The §15.1 tenant suspend drain force-terminates every non-terminal session, `suspended` included, so the resume driver and the leader backlog re-check cannot revive a suspended tenant's session. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/tenant_suspend.go:179-203

CODE-6 item 7 **MCP** moves the chain ahead of Classify. Keep that ordering: a fix that drops it reopens a content-policy bypass for held messages. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/mcptools/mcptools_register.go:376-410
DECISION: Filed no security findings in round 45. BECAUSE the controls this proposal touches are either held or settled by the owner. Held: the D1 lease gate fails closed on a read error, credential delivery isolation and the §4.9 pre-claim check apply to the checkpoint resume, `admitResume` fails closed, and every quota, retry, and age bound is Postgres-sourced. Settled by the owner: suspended sessions exempt from caps including held-pod (OD-S10), no cap check on a failure edge (OD-S9), and the runtime `busy` veto on idle suspension (Decision 30). SPEC-5o accepts budget-key expiry outside `suspended`. — ALTERNATIVES: considered filing the held-pod pod-exhaustion window and the runtime `busy` self-report as trust-boundary findings, and rejected both as owner decisions with no security bound sourced from the pod.

### [spec.46.review-security.1]
DECISION: filed nothing under the security lens in spec round 46 — BECAUSE the only staged-spec delta since round 45 is the SPEC-1b **Exits from `resuming`.** same-replica cancel sentence, which adds a cancel to an existing control and removes none, and a whole-staging walk found no regressed control and no security bound sourced from a pod self-report — ALTERNATIVES considered and rejected: (1) the §15.1 tenant-suspend row's "All active sessions in the tenant are drained" read against SPEC-6a's cap exclusion of `suspended` (SPEC-6a never calls a `suspended` session inactive, and the drain predates 0092; spec/15_external-api-surface.md:848); (2) a podless `suspended` session resumed by the leader re-check during a `DeleteByUser` job (the §12.8 Article 18 restriction gates only creation and lets in-flight sessions run, so this predates 0092; spec/12_storage-architecture.md:807); (3) a resume's credentials reaching a pod after a terminal write on another replica (owner-settled OD-3/OD-T2; SPEC-1g makes the credential service refuse a revoked credential, and §11.4 revocation cancels every non-terminal session).
FACT: SPEC-5b step 2 replaces §6.2's "releases the pod back to the pool" with the pool's session-end disposition (recycle or drain). Under §5.2 a recycle runs the per-slot cleanup and the whole-pod scrub and keeps the tenant pin, so the release tightens one-session-only reuse rather than relaxing it — EVIDENCE: spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:229, spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:443, :462, :480
FACT: the `PreMessageDelivery` phase fires "before the gateway delivers an inter-session message ... to the target session". A held DLQ message passes it before the hold, which matches the non-spec CODE-6 item 7 ordering — EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:1148
